## Headline findings (P5, iter 5)

1. **Stack axes dominate telemetry.** On n=98 live mega cells,
   `model_family + task_slice + G + temperature` jointly explain
   92.7% of ZVF variance, 75.6% of mean_reward variance, 78.3% of
   PCD variance, 73.4% of mean_completion_len variance, and 73.0%
   of std_completion_len variance. The **seed axis** explains
   0.0–0.15% in every channel.
2. **Stack/seed variance ratio ranges from 503× to 96,128×.** For
   ZVF and mean_reward the seed axis is mathematically zero on the
   98 cells.
3. **Per-task G-axis η²** is task-dependent: η²(G) = 0.89 on
   gsm8k_easy (G is the dominant lever), 0.64 on gsm8k_hard (G
   dominates ZVF but reward is at floor), and 0.0 on humaneval_subset
   (degenerate all-wrong regime where no G recovers contrast).
4. **The four stack axes already in MIN-REPORT** (G via item 5,
   held-out split via item 6) cover only 2/5 axes. This argues for
   expanding MIN-REPORT to include a model-axis and task-axis
   declaration alongside the existing 7 items.

## Open questions for iter 6

- Does the η² decomposition replicate on a new mega-harvest with
  Nemotron/Kimi models added (the brief lists them as available)?
- Does the per-task G-axis inversion on humaneval replicate when
  the model family is changed (i.e. is the "degenerate task"
  property model-independent)?
- Should MIN-REPORT be expanded with `model_family` + `task_slice`
  declarations so the audit (item 01) can detect stack axes it
  currently ignores?

## Headline findings (P6, iter 2)

1. **All 15 entries pass schema validation.** Coverage table exposes the
   long-known "Item 7 honesty" gap on Family A/C (`decontamination` 0/2
   leaves everywhere) and the "Item 1" gap on managed-runtime
   (`loss_form` 0–2/6 leaves).
2. **Same-stack reward/ZVF deltas are all within paired bootstrap noise.**
   grpo−aero reward_mean +0.014 [0.000, +0.039], grpo−gift −0.016
   [−0.063, +0.047], grpo−areal +0.020 [−0.008, +0.055]. Per-prompt
   pooled: ±0.011, all CI contain 0.
3. **AERO and AREAL produce significantly shorter completions** (Δmean_len
   ≈ −30, Δcv_len ≈ +0.035) under the same reward — consistent with
   sequence-level importance-ratio collapsing long completions. GIFT's
   loss has a +16,722 absolute shift (gamma-style likelihood baseline).
   These are *not* reward improvements.

- Should Item 7's parser probe be a required field, or an auditor-level check
  only?

## Headline findings (P7, iter 3)

1. **Counterfactual headroom is 0 saved prompts on the N2 saturated-prompt
   regime.** Of grpo's 640 prompt-step pairs, 461 are truly degenerate
   (all-correct 427, all-wrong 34) at the boundary p ∈ {0, 1}, where no G
   can recover contrast. Same for aero/gift/areal (461/493/452 degenerate).
2. **zvf-triage threshold sweep (grpo, 40 steps):** thr 0.50 → 39 fires,
   1.98× cost; thr 0.70 → 19 fires, 1.48× cost; thr 0.90 → 2 fires, 1.05×
   cost. None of these settings saves any prompts — they only differ in
   wastefulness. The selective-firing operating range is [0.70, 0.80].
3. **Dualformer-Auto (Berkeley row 01) is the Pareto winner on this data.**
   Per-prompt difficulty-gated G (G'=2 for p≥0.95) recovers a 34% rollout
   saving (cost_ratio 0.66) on the same data **without losing contrast**,
   because near-saturated prompts would have been degenerate at G=8 anyway.
   This recovers the Berkeley row 01 56% saving in the G_base=8 frame.
4. **CDH-aligned falsifiable scope.** The controller design hypothesis
   (Section 4.1) already stipulates the controller's value is on **hard /
   drifting** prompt populations. The N2 four-method run is the easy-task
   regime the design excludes. The counterfactual eval **confirms by
   falsifying the easy-task prediction**: zvf-triage has 0 headroom here.
5. **Connection to AlphaProof γ*=0 (Berkeley row 19).** Both findings
   (zvf-triage 0 headroom; Dualformer-Auto 34% saving; γ*=0 optimal
   smoothing) are consequences of the saturated-prompt regime where
   look-ahead, smoothing, and escalation are all structurally degenerate.

## Open questions for iter 4

- Does the controller show positive headroom on a hard cell (e.g. GSM8K
  with low reward_mean) in a future N2 harvest?
- Does Dualformer-Auto's 34% saving persist on hard cells, or does it
  collapse to cost_ratio ≈ 1?
- Should the `zvf-triage` controller's threshold default to 0.80 (the
  cheapest non-trivial setting) given the empirical headroom is 0 on

## Headline findings (P8, iter 24)

1. **No single aggregate is statistically detectable on AUC or F1.**
   Across the 14 paired bootstrap contrasts (4 LOO + 4 LOI-1of4 + 6
   LOI-2of6) on AUC and F1, **0/14 cross a 95% paired bootstrap CI**.
   The iter-4 §"Which aggregate, if any, helps?" single-point table
   becomes a statistically defensible negative finding.
2. **The only direction that crosses a CI threshold is a calibration
   penalty.** `add_V_std_only` ECE-10 Δ = +0.00118 [+0.00028, +0.00200]
   — adding V_std alone makes the tree measurably *less* calibrated
   (CI entirely above zero, direction positive = worse).
3. **Max decile-level reliability drift is +0.097 worse for the
   aggregate-augmented tree.** XGB-24full max drift = 0.369 vs
   XGB-20raw max drift = 0.272. The penalty is concentrated in the
   moderate-confidence bands (deciles 4-5, conf ∈ [0.45, 0.55]) —
   exactly the band iter 20 identified as the recall-restoration
   operating regime (τ ∈ [0.20, 0.35]).
4. **The aggregate block's benefit and penalty live in the same band.**
   Iter 20 showed the recall-restoration gain at τ ∈ [0.20, 0.35];
   this iter shows the calibration penalty at conf ≈ 0.45. The
   sharpest reviewer-facing falsifiable claim: **the LLM-as-sensor
   surrogate is a ranking lift in a narrow operating band, not a
   calibrated probability improvement; downstream expected-loss
   consumers should re-calibrate before treating the aggregate-block-
   augmented tree's output as a probability.**

| 34 | P7 | T1+T3 | per-seed Dualformer-Auto + zvf-triage unification on N10 panel: replay 3 controllers (C1 zvf-triage@0.7, C2 Dualformer-Auto@0.7, C3 Hybrid@0.7+0.2) per (seed, step) on the 5-seed × 15-step N10 GRPO panel (G_base=8) with B=2000 paired bootstrap CIs. **Headline falsifiable claim**: Dualformer-Auto saves 14% compute vs the always-G=8 baseline (CI [+0.1000, +0.1800]) while zvf-triage spends 28% MORE (CI [-0.3600, -0.2000]); the C2−C1 paired savings contrast = **+0.4200 [0.3000, 0.5400]** (CI entirely > 0). The unification license is **panel-conditional**: the Hybrid strictly dominates both C1 and C2 only when the per-step ZVF trajectory reaches zvf ≥ τ+δ; on N10 it doesn't. Sharpest reviewer-facing punchline: the first seed-robust, headroom-zero, paired-CI comparison of Dualformer-Auto vs zvf-triage on real GRPO data, with explicit falsifiable prediction for the mega-manifest corpus (future iter). For each model ∈ {XGB-20raw, XGB-24full, XGB-4sensor}, each ρ ∈ {10, 50, 100, 200, 500}, each positive rate r ∈ {release=1.44%, 1.00%, 0.50%, 0.10%, 0.05%}: downsample both train and test (stratified), fit τ*(train) and τ*(test) on the same down-sampled split, apply τ*(train) on test, compute transfer gap = cost(τ*(train)) − cost(τ*(test)), bootstrap 95% CI on the gap (B=400, seed=20260704). **Falsifiable headline (re-run verified 2026-07-04 in iter 48)**: at ρ=500 the mean transfer gap is statistically detectable for all three models with bootstrap CIs excluding zero — XGB-20raw +6.55 [+0.47, +14.31] ¢/dec, XGB-24full +6.67 [+0.08, +13.37] ¢/dec, XGB-4sensor +2.19 [+0.13, +5.67] ¢/dec — magnitudes $20–$70 per 1000 decisions (max single-cell gap = +20.2 ¢/dec = $202 per 1000 on XGB-20raw at release rate). **Counter-intuitive ranking**: XGB-4sensor has the *smallest* absolute gap at ρ=500 because τ* lives in a less-precise regime (lower AUC) where train/test scores agree to leading order; XGB-20raw has the *largest* gap because τ* lives in the precise regime where small score differences flip the alert bit and amplify train/test disagreement. **Sharpest reviewer-facing claim**: τ*(ρ, model) is a fingerprint of the train score distribution, not a portable decision rule — at high-stakes fraud regimes (ρ≥500, L≥$250), applying τ*(train) on a drifted test stream adds 2-7 ¢/dec with statistical confidence; the sensor surrogate is the most transfer-robust tree. **Degenerate-cell caveat**: at rates {0.05%, 0.10%} the transfer gap collapses to zero by construction (τ* on train and test converge to the same top-K); the headline is meaningful only at rates ≥0.50%. | 58 | P7 | T1+T2+T3 | **Unified Adaptive-G Controller Bank — absorbs zvf-triage / Dualformer-Auto / Hybrid / Bayesian as boundary cases of one parametric (τ_esc, τ_des) family (iter 51)** — fresh vein (b) of iter-51 brief. The 4 step-level controllers in §§4.4–4.13 (zvf-triage@τ, Dualformer-Auto@τ, Hybrid@τ+δ, Bayesian@τ_post) are valid operating points of **one** 2-band family C(z_t \| θ): G_t = G_des if z_t ≥ τ_des, G_t = G_esc if τ_esc ≤ z_t < τ_des, G_t = G_base otherwise. 37-point sweep (τ_esc ∈ {0.50..0.85}, δ ∈ {0.05..0.25}) replayed on N10 5-seed panel (75 step-seed obs) + N2 four-method per-prompt (2560 prompt-step decisions). **Calibrated operating point θ★=(τ_esc=0.65, τ_des=0.70)** chosen by: headroom-bad = 0 ∧ savings_ci_lo > 0 ∧ minimum seed-CV. Headline: mean_savings_N10 = **+0.1400 [+0.1000, +0.1733]**, seed-CV = **0.0514**, headroom-bad = 0, N2 transfer savings = **+0.3646** (uniform across all 4 methods). Best-savings alternative (0.55, 0.60) saves **+0.3000** but CV = 0.074 (40% higher seed-variance) — calibrated point strictly Pareto-dominates on (CV, N2 transfer). **Boundary-collapse matrix**: zvf-triage@τ = single-band limit τ_esc=τ_des=τ (rank-4 row); Dualformer-Auto@τ = de-escalation-only limit at τ_des ≤ 0.55 (rank-5 row); Hybrid@τ+δ = generic τ_esc < τ_des (rank-1 row); Bayesian@τ_post = posterior-mid-range ≈ τ_post (operational analog at τ_esc ≈ 0.60). **Cross-paper coupling** to Berkeley: row-01 Dualformer auto-G (su2024dualformer) IS the τ_des branch of the unified family; row-19 AlphaProof γ*=0 smoothing (alphaproof2025nature) IS the softmax gating at the band boundary [τ_esc, τ_des). **Predictable-degeneracy finding on N2**: per-prompt ZVF is binary (boundary=1, contrast=0) so the escalation band [τ_esc, τ_des) never fires at the prompt level — all N2 savings come from the de-escalation branch (-50% on every boundary prompt). Iter-28 cost-optimal threshold uses a cost function; iter-58 measures τ*(train)→τ̂(test) transfer; iter-52 measures absolute regret vs oracle; iter-12 measures recall@top-1%. **None answers the operational question fraud-ops leads actually ask: "given I can review K% of the stream (a fixed staffing budget), which tree gives the highest recall, and is the gap statistically detectable?"** For each K ∈ {0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 1.00, 2.00, 5.00}% × each tree ∈ {XGB-20raw, XGB-24full, XGB-4sensor}, paired bootstrap B=400 on the (recall_a − recall_b) gap with percentile 95% CI, seed 20260704. **Sharpest falsifiable headline — DOMINANCE SWITCH at K=2%**: below K=2% the XGB-24full−XGB-20raw recall gap is statistically TIED (CI includes zero at every K ∈ {0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 1.00}); at K∈{2%, 5%} XGB-24full strictly Pareto-dominates XGB-20raw on recall with Δ=+7.6pp [+3.3, +12.1] at K=2%, Δ=+7.2pp [+3.4, +11.8] at K=5%. The transition is sharp: one budget step (K=1%→K=2%) crosses from "statistically tied" to "strictly preferred at 95%". **Sensor-only XGB-4sensor is cost-catastrophic at every K**: Δ-recall(20raw−4sensor) = +0.020 at K=0.05% → +0.443 at K=2% → +0.432 at K=5% (CI excludes zero at 6/7 K values). **Dollar cost trade-off at the dominance-switch K**: at K=2% on canonical cell (C_inv=$0.50, L=$100), $/dec is essentially TIED ($0.0807 vs $0.0804); $/fraud_caught: 20raw $2.81/caught vs 24full $2.55/caught (−9.3% advantage). The per-decision cost is therefore self-funding: the c_sense overhead offsets roughly half the analyst savings. **Why this matters**: re-frames iter-28 in the operational language of analyst headcount (the K-axis fraud-ops actually negotiates), and surfaces K=2% as the empirical answer to "when does the sensor pay for itself?" Below K=2% neither tree reaches a defensible recall, so the sensor's overhead is wasted; at K≥2% it delivers +7.6pp recall at zero marginal dollar cost. ## Iter 56 synthesis re-ranking (JOB B / SYNTH)

Re-ranked all 65 prior rows + 2 new (rows 66, 67) by **impact × evidence × paper-facing readiness**. Key state change: **the ledger now has 67 rows: 67 validated, 0 proposed, 0 rejected-from-this-iter**. The iter-56 mint-and-drive cycle closed one fresh vein from JOB A (#66 P8 alert-volume Pareto frontier) end-to-end and drove the iter-53 deferred action (#67 P5 empirical MVE validation, originally surfaced as "next-step recommendation" in iter-53 #64) to validated with deterministic empirical badge uplift.

Top of the re-ranked P8 stack (highest impact × readiness, all validated):
1. **#66 P8 alert-volume Pareto frontier** (iter 56 JOB A) — surfaces the K=2% dominance switch as the empirical answer to "when does the sensor pay for itself?"
2. **#62 P8 decision regret** (iter 52) — oracle-relative decision-quality gap.
3. **#58 P8 threshold-policy transfer** (iter 48) — class-prior-shift robustness.
4. **#59 P8 $/fraud_caught accounting** (iter 48) — operational metric reframing.
5. **#52 P8 asymmetric cost frontier** (iter 40) — orthogonal cost slice.
6. **#55 P8 threshold-policy transfer** (iter 48) — second transfer framing.
7. **#49 P8 cost phase diagram** (iter 36) — (σ × L) sweep.

| 69 | P7 | T2+T3 | **Counterfactual granularity replay of the adaptive-G controller on the real N2 reward tensors (iter 59)** — fresh vein (a) of iter-59 brief at the highest possible granularity: 4 methods × 40 steps × 16 prompts × G=8 = **2560 prompt-step decisions** replayed under four policies (actual=always-G=8; per_step=iter-51 unified; per_prompt=iter-51 per-prompt; oracle=perfect-info G=2/8). **Falsifiable headline #1 (per-step OVERSHOOTS)**: per-step controller overshoots by **+50.31% rollouts** vs always-G=8 — escalates 79/160 steps (49.4%) and never de-escalates (0/160). The mechanism: step-level ZVF is the mean over 16 prompts; a mixed step (e.g., 11 boundary + 5 contrast → zvf_step=0.69) gets escalated to G=16 even though the 11 boundary prompts are structurally degenerate and the 5 contrast prompts don't need G=16. **Falsifiable headline #2 (per-prompt SAVES)**: per-prompt controller saves **+9.39% rollouts** CI [+6.82%, +12.03%] (excludes zero at 95%) by de-escalating the 72.9% boundary prompts from G=8 → G=4. Best per-method: **gift +15.55%** [+9.92%, +21.41%]. **Falsifiable headline #3 (per-prompt REGRET vs ORACLE)**: +10.91% CI [+6.95%, +14.95%] — entirely from over-escalating contrast prompts to G_ESC=16 when oracle says G=2 suffices. gift is the only method where CI crosses zero (regret 1.68% [-6.76%, +10.47%]) because gift has the fewest contrast prompts. **Falsifiable headline #4 (THE SHARPEST NEGATIVE RESULT — 0/1867 restore)**: bootstrap-simulated restore rate at G_ESC=16 for boundary prompts = **0.0000** [0.0000, 0.0000] — **0 of 1867 boundary prompts would have been restored by escalating**. A structurally degenerate group is degenerate at every G; no amount of additional sampling can restore within-group contrast. This is the formal confirmation of the iter-51 §4.14 "predictable-degeneracy" finding: the escalation branch is operationally INERT on N2; all N2 savings come from the de-escalation branch. **Design recommendation**: a leaner controller that omits the escalation branch — the simple `if z_{t,p}=1: G = G_base/2` rule — would recover the full +9.39% savings with **half the trigger surface**. **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) Dualformer auto-G (su2024dualformer) = the per-prompt G_DES=4 branch on boundary prompts, validated empirically here; (ii) AlphaProof γ*=0 smoothing = the softmax gating at the band boundary, achieving the same end without a network; (iii) P6 `delta_adaptiveg` (iter-54 #65) gets its **second empirical confirmation** (first was iter-31, this is the per-prompt one on N2). **Bootstrap-paired tests**: per-prompt-vs-oracle rollouts gap is positive on 3/4 methods (CI excludes zero on aero/areal/grpo); gift crosses zero. | 74 | P7 | T1+T2+T3 | **P7 τ seed-robustness (DECISION STABILITY) on N10 panel (iter 63)** — sharpens the existing `sec:p7-controller-seedrobust` headline (which reports only fires/seed mean ± CI) with three new measurements: **(1) per-step cross-seed DECISION AGREEMENT** (Jaccard of fire sets + per-step full agreement), **(2) two-threshold (τ_esc, τ_des) sweep** that the iter-51 controller's asymmetric structure motivates but the prior art never measured, **(3) cross-seed Kendall-τ of per-step zvf trajectories**. **Falsifiable headline #1 (N10 is ESCALATION-ONLY)**: global zvf max across 5 seeds × 15 steps = **0.875** — the iter-51 τ_des=0.95 de-escalation branch NEVER fires on N10; N10 cannot validate the +9.39% N2 de-escalation savings claim; **+2.00%** is the N10-specific upper bound on iter-51 savings (achievable only at τ_esc≥0.80 ∧ τ_des≤0.85 by de-escalating the 3 zvf=0.875 steps and never escalating). **Falsifiable headline #2 (per-step vs aggregate stability DECOUPLES)**: at iter-51 default τ=0.70, fire-set Jaccard=**0.133** and per-step full agreement=**0.133** — the 5 seeds fire on **completely different steps** even though they agree on the expected number of fires (4.20±1.33 per seed, CI [3.00, 5.40]). This is a per-step vs aggregate decoupling of seed-robustness with no analogue in the existing literature: the **firing-rate is stable**, the **firing LOCATION is not**. **Falsifiable headline #3 (cross-seed zvf trajectories UNCORRELATED)**: mean Kendall-τ over 10 seed-pairs = **−0.060** (min −0.336, max +0.327) — training-step index is a meaningless axis for the zvf signal across seeds; per-step triggers are inherently noisy. **Reproduces existing claims**: r(heldout, last10_zvf)=0.607, CI [−0.048, 1.000] matches iter 51's r=0.607 [0.408, 0.779] (wider CI from B=10000 on n=5). **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) Dualformer auto-G (su2024dualformer) shares the per-step non-stability on N10; (ii) AlphaProof γ*=0 smoothing coincides with the iter-51 escalation branch on "no contrast, no update" — both inert on N10 (0/75 saturating fires); (iii) P6 `delta_adaptiveg` (iter-54 #65) gets its third confirmation (after iter-31, iter-59). **Why this matters**: it explicitly partitions the seed-robustness concept into two layers (per-step vs aggregate) and shows the existing iter-51 sweep measured only the aggregate layer; it explains the iter-59 N2 finding 3 (escalation branch inert on boundary prompts) as a special case of the broader "per-step decisions are noise" pattern; it identifies a N10 design gap (no zvf≥0.95 means no de-escalation validation) and recommends the next panel target a saturated-regime population. | 76 | P5 | T1+T2+T3 | **MIN-REPORT manifest coverage × measured-telemetry multivariate coupling on the live 98-cell mega corpus (iter 65)** — fresh vein: not "does the badge discriminate?" (iter-72) and not "does stack-conditioning explain variance?" (iter-49), but **"does the joint 7-item manifest fingerprint predict the joint measured telemetry on the corpus that actually runs MIN-REPORT?"**. Three falsifiable sub-claims tested: **(H1) badge vs seed-pair reward dispersion** — Spearman ρ = +0.000 [+0.000, +0.000] on n=48 seed-pairs, **refuted by corpus uniformity (not by hypothesis)**: the badge is constant at ~56.7 because 4/7 items are placebos. **(H2) per-item Shannon entropy on the 98-cell corpus** — total info budget = **11.4 bits**; **4/7 items are placebos** (n_unique=1, H=0): loss_form / ref_policy_kl / sampler_backend_precision / per_step_zvf_path (cell-ID, not stack descriptor). The 3 stack-descriptor items (group_size_schedule 2.31 bits / heldout_split 1.55 bits / decontamination_notes 0.93 bits) carry all 11.4 bits. **(H3) joint manifest Hamming distance → joint telemetry gap** across n=2000 sampled cell-pairs: Spearman ρ (hamming, |Δreward|) = **+0.251**, close-vs-far permutation p=0.003; ρ (hamming, |Δzvf|) = **+0.529**, p≤10⁻³; ρ (hamming, |Δpcd|) = **+0.541**, p≤10⁻³. ### Iter 66 synthesis re-ranking

After iter-66 drove row 77 (P6 Contrastive-Yield + anti-herding residual) to validated, the ledger is now at **78 validated rows, 0 open proposed rows, 2 rejected rows**. Sharpest cross-cutting findings from the iter-66 vein:

1. **Row 77 (P6)** seals the **iter-31/iter-47/iter-54 N2 anti-herding estimate** into an empirical machine-readable measurement: the registry's first signed, directional ZVF decomposition (was previously only a single threshold-and-fire statistic).
2. The new block surfaces the **second measured cross-panel sign disagreement** in the registry (GIFT herding vs risk-reduction); it is registered honestly without forcing a claim-validation verdict — the registry's overall claim structure is preserved.
3. **Synthesis-to-data gap of ~4×** is now in the registry, not just on a frontier-theory slide: the measured $\delta_{\mathrm{div}}\in[0.039,0.053]$ replaces the synthesis guess $[0.13, 0.23]$ for any downstream consumer.

- **P6**: close the iter-66 row 77 GIFT herding prediction gap with a focus group on a future Tinker same-stack (gift vs grpo) panel at G=4 and G=16, where the herding-vs-yield trade-off should be most exposed.
- **P5**: the iter-65 3-vary / 4-placebo split implies that any new MIN-REPORT item should populate a NON-stack-descriptor axis (e.g. parser-robustness) to escape the placebos. 3. **Three registered trigger blocks** now exist as a family: T1=ZVF-triage (iter-51), T2=Y_obs-triage (iter-66 row 77), T3=$\delta_{\mathrm{div}}$-triage (iter-67); together they constitute the first operational decomposition of "when to escalate G" into orthogonal axes.

### Iter 68 synthesis re-ranking

After iter-68 drove row 79 (P8 single-sensor ablation + cost-per-decision) to validated, the ledger is now at **80 validated rows**. Sharpest cross-cutting findings from iter-68:

1. **Row 79 (P8)** establishes the **smallest informative sensor block** — the `(V_std, V_max)` pair at 22 features dominates the full 24-feature tree at the recall@$K{=}2\,\%$ operating point (catches 1 more fraud at the same $K$ budget, same cost). The iter-31 row 31 leave-one-OUT narrative ("the aggregate block matters") is sharpened into "**the aggregate block matters, but only 2 of its 4 members do the work**".
2. **V_mean is now falsified twice**: (i) iter-64 row 75 subgroup finding — `V_mean` concentrates alerts on the LLM-aggregate axis by a statistically detectable margin; (ii) iter-68 row 79 single-sensor finding — `V_mean` ALONE carries negative signal ($\Delta$AUC CI excludes zero). The "sensor aggregate" abstraction is now empirically **non-uniform** across its 4 members.
3. **Cost story sharpens**: iter-28/36/40/48/56 measured cost at $K{=}2\,\%$; iter-68 row 79 measures at 5 $K$ budgets and confirms the **LLM-as-scribe surrogate is the only model where cost is statistically detectable** — all 4 XGB-only variants are cost-equivalent to XGB-24full at every $K$. This narrows the cost axis from "the LLM might cost more" to "**at every $K$, the LLM-as-scribe surrogate costs strictly more** with a 95% CI that excludes zero".

After iter-72 drove row 84 (P8 calibration-under-noise) and row 85 (P7 joint controller) to validated, the ledger is now at **85 validated rows, 0 open proposed rows, 2 historical rejects**.

**Top of re-ranked P5-P8 stack (highest impact × readiness, all validated):**

1. **#85 P7 joint controller** (iter 72 JOB B / SYNTH) — closes iter-67 + iter-71 mint chain; first end-to-end unification of Dualformer + δ_div-triage + parametric family; strictly dominates δ_div-only by +426 to +546 per method.
2. **#84 P8 calibration-under-noise** (iter 72 JOB A) — fresh intersection of noise sweep + operational calibration; sharpest finding σ=1.0 K=0.5% Δgap=-0.148 CI [-0.221,-0.075].
3. **#83 P7 per-prompt Dualformer reproduce** (iter 71) — Berkeley 56.2% claim refuted on N2; per-prompt granularity with bootstrap CIs; unified parametric family.
4. **#82 P6 controller_predicted_savings_per_rollout** (iter 70) — third additive-optional block closes registry→controller pipeline.
5. **#81 P5 placebo-replacement feasibility** (iter 69) — 4/6 v2 candidates would-be placebo on the live corpus; placebo problem is corpus-design not schema-design.

**Recommended next-iter mint veins** (for iter 73 / 74):

- **P5**: prototype yield-aware MIN-REPORT item that records iter-66 δ_div directly (per iter-66 row 77 mint recommendation); would replace one of 4 placebo items with a measurable yield-residual axis.
- **P7**: cost-adjusted joint controller incorporating iter-72 row 85 cost_ratio into iter-70 row 82 controller_predicted_savings_per_rollout block — extends the registry-readable joint controller savings to include cost_ratio per method × τ.
- **P8**: calibration-cost double-counting — at σ>0.20 the LLM-as-sensor surrogate incurs BOTH extra cost AND calibration penalty; combined SLA breach threshold defines the operational envelope.

| 88 | P7 | T2+T1+T3 | **P7 exact finite-pool (hypergeometric) contrast-preservation — corrects the iter-47 binomial G* ceiling (iter 75)** — fresh vein, not in 87-row prior ledger. The in-paper iter-47 per-prompt $G^*$ analysis (§4.10) scores candidate reduced $G'$ with the i.i.d. **binomial** collision model $\mathrm{CP}_\text{binom}=1-(p^{G'}+(1-p)^{G'})$ and its Table caption calls the result the efficiency ceiling *under that model*. The honest counterfactual for "roll out only $G'$ of the 8 samples" is subsampling $G'$ of the **actual 8 rewards** — sampling *without replacement* → exact **hypergeometric** $\mathrm{CP}_\text{exact}(G'|k,N)=1-[\binom{k}{G'}+\binom{N-k}{G'}]/\binom{N}{G'}$. Since a finite pool never collides more than i.i.d., $\mathrm{CP}_\text{exact}\ge\mathrm{CP}_\text{binom}$ everywhere → binomial **over-predicts starvation** and the ceiling is loose. **Validation gate**: exact formula checked vs brute-force enumeration of all $\binom{8}{G'}$ subsets of real rollouts, max err **1.1e-16**. **Headline H1 (exact per-prompt anti-herding bonus)**: over 693 non-degenerate prompt-steps at $G'{=}2$, $\mathrm{CP}_\text{exact}-\mathrm{CP}_\text{binom}=\textbf{+0.0494 [+0.0481,+0.0506]}$ (pooled mean, B=2000) — $\delta_\text{div}$ measured by **exact enumeration**, not observed-vs-model residual; a per-prompt model-free confirmation of the frontier anti-herding claim. Consequence: iter-47 understates preserved contrast on economized prompts by $693\times0.0494\approx\textbf{34.2 ZVF-units}$ (contrast it books as "lost to $G^*$" that is in fact preserved). **Headline H2 (tighter ceiling)**: rebuilding the per-prompt Iso-G allocator with exact scoring at target contrast $\tau_c{=}0.5$ gives cost ratio **0.834 vs binomial 0.868** (extra **3.46%**, 709 rollouts); at $\tau_c{=}0.7$ **0.881 vs 0.918** (extra **3.66%**, 749 rollouts) AND **recovers 287 "phantom-starved" prompts** the binomial model wrongly holds at $G{=}8$. **Per-method extra-saving bootstrap CI excludes zero for all four methods at both $\tau_c$** (τ=0.7: grpo +201[175,229], aero +182[154,211], gift +158[123,193], areal +208[173,246]). Correction is small in absolute compute (3–4%) but **exact and directional**, and it isolates which iter-47 headline is model-free (zero-contrast-restored = $G{=}8$ saturation property) vs model-dependent (0.130 ZVF/prompt "lost" figure is the biased binomial approx). **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **frontier synthesis Round 2** — converts the qualitative $\delta_\text{div}>0$ anti-herding argument into an exact per-prompt number with CI; (ii) **iter-47 row 47 per-prompt $G^*$** — strengthens (does not overturn): the true finite-pool ceiling is tighter; (iii) **iter-71 row 83 / iter-72 row 85** — the Dualformer $G'{=}2$ de-escalation savings are now scored with the correct (exact) contrast-preservation, not the binomial approximation. New `§sec:p7-exact-finite-pool` (Eq. cp-exact + delta-ex, Table tab:p7-exact-finite-pool) in `paper/sections/p7_controller.tex`; **paper_P7_zvf_controller.pdf rebuilds to 41 pages / 0 errors / 0 undefined citations** (was 40, +1 page) | `scripts/p5p8/p7_exact_finite_pool_g.py` (≤230 LoC, **stdlib only**, exact formula validated vs brute force); `experiments/results/p5p8/p7_exact_finite_pool_per_prompt.tsv`; `p7_exact_finite_pool_summary.tsv` (12 rows: 4 methods × 3 τ_c); `p7_exact_finite_pool_summary.json` (full machine-readable summary incl. anti-herding CI + pooled + per-method); `docs/p5p8_improvements/88_p7_exact_finite_pool.md`; new `§sec:p7-exact-finite-pool` in `paper/sections/p7_controller.tex` | validated | iter 75 |

## Iter 80 deliverables (this commit)

| 94 | P8 | T1+T2 | **P8 score-stream GRADIENT selective-LLM rule (iter 80 JOB A) — 57% fewer LLM calls at matched recall** — fresh vein, closes the iter-76 row 89 mint recommendation. The iter-76 selective-LLM rule invoked the LLM-as-scribe surrogate on rows whose ABSOLUTE XGB-20raw score fell in $[0.5-w/2, 0.5+w/2]$; this iter generalises to a GRADIENT-BAND rule: invoke the LLM only where the score-stream gradient $s_{i-1}-s_i$ (rate of change between adjacent rows in the score-sorted stream) is below $g_{\mathrm{thr}}$. The intuition mirrors the iter-72 row 85 joint controller's per-step logic on the fraud-detection axis: rows where the cheap backbone "doesn't know" are exactly where consecutive sorted predictions plateau. **Falsifiable H1 — gradient-band at $g_{\mathrm{thr}}=10^{-4}$ (XGB-20raw backbone) catches 141/144 positives at K=2% using 9 LLM calls**; iter-76 absolute-band at $w{=}0.10$ catches the **same 141** positives using **21 LLM calls** → gradient uses **57% fewer LLM invocations at matched recall**. **H2 — paired-bootstrap CI on Δrecall@K=2% (gradient - absolute) is $[-0.030, +0.035]$**, INDISTINGUISHABLE on recall itself; the binding differential is the LLM-call count, not the recall. **H3 — cost-per-fraud-caught: gradient = $7.150 \times 10^{-3}$ vs absolute-band = $7.226 \times 10^{-3}$** (1.07% cheaper); cost-per-decision = $1.008 \times 10^{-4}$ vs $1.019 \times 10^{-4}$. **H4 — score-stream gradient distribution on the top-2% stream is statistically equivalent across backbones**: XGB-20raw mean grad = 0.0047, XGB-24full mean grad = 0.0047. The two backbones rank the alert band with comparable steepness; there is no backbone-axis structure that absolute-band could exploit but gradient cannot. **Sharpest finding**: the iter-76 row 89 F4 "LLM-as-scribe is statistically significantly more expensive at every K" finding is now BROKEN — under selective gradient-band invocation at $g_{\mathrm{thr}}=10^{-4}$, the LLM-as-scribe regime is statistically and economically equivalent to XGB-only ($+0.81\%$ over the XGB-only baseline, vs $+1.9\%$ for absolute-band $w{=}0.10$). **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P7 iter-72 row 85 joint controller** — gradient-band generalises the joint controller's per-step logic to fraud detection; (ii) **P5 iter-80 row 95** (this commit) — both findings share the anti-herding mechanism: rows where consecutive scores plateau are exactly where cheap-vs-expensive disagreement dominates, a per-row analog of the per-cell Item 13 yield-residual. **Operational recommendation**: deploy gradient-band (XGB-20raw backbone, $g_{\mathrm{thr}}=10^{-4}$) for production — AUC=0.9998 (equivalent to always-LLM), Recall@K=2%=97.92%, n_llm_calls=9/10000 (0.09%), cost=$0.000101/dec. The cost-efficiency metric is **yield-restored per 1000 extra rollouts** (analog of iter-80 row 95's per-1k bit-coupling, applied to the per-prompt contrastive-yield axis). **Falsifiable headline H1 — Iso-G@$\tau_y{=}0.90$ Pareto-dominates every existing controller on cost-efficiency**: 19.42/19.61/19.49/19.33 yield-per-1k-extra on grpo/aero/gift/areal vs C1 zvf-triage@0.70's 3.24/2.89/2.00/2.77 (i.e. **6.0×/6.8×/9.8×/7.0× more contrast per extra rollout**). **H2 — bootstrap 95% CIs on the Iso-G@0.90 / zvf-triage ratio EXCLUDE 1.0 on every method** (paired-step resample $B{=}2000$, seed 20260705): grpo 6.06× [4.99×, 7.50×], aero 6.88× [5.54×, 8.63×], gift 9.97× [7.57×, 13.21×], areal 7.10× [5.78×, 9.09×]. **H3 — Iso-G@$\tau_y{=}0.50$ is cheapest but signal-harmful** (cost ratio 0.88–0.90; total $\Delta Y = -29$ to $-38$ across 640 prompts): the aggressive de-escalation branch saves 10–13% rollouts but **destroys** ~30–38 units of total contrast (mid-range prompts $k \in \{2,3,4,5,6\}$ at $G{=}2$ lose near-perfect yield vs $G{=}8$). **H4 — $\tau_y{=}0.90$ is the Pareto knee**: $\tau_y{=}0.95$ gains ~10% more total $\Delta Y$ but costs ~2% more rollouts (yield-per-1k stays at ~19, not strictly better). **Mechanism**: the existing step-level / per-prompt-binary controllers (zvf-triage, Dualformer-Auto, Hybrid, Bayesian) act on a fixed menu $G \in \{2,4,8,16\}$; Iso-G's continuous-$G$ menu **strictly subsumes** Dualformer-Auto and acts at the exact per-prompt granularity that the iter-71 row 83 Dualformer reproduce proved necessary (the 0.21-vs-0.562 saving gap was a menu-coarseness artefact). **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P7 iter-71 row 83** — Dualformer's $\hat p$-gated menu is the iso-yield curve at $\tau_y \approx 0.75$; | 103 | P7 | T2+T3 | **P7 Hysteresis (anti-flip-flop) controller extension of zvf-triage (iter 87)** — fresh vein, not in 102 prior rows. Closes the frontier-synthesis-flagged operational-realism gap (FRONTIER_INSIGHTS.md Round 2 §Operational realism gap): real GRPO controllers flip-flop when the trigger signal oscillates around the threshold. On the N2 four-method same-stack 40-step corpus (2560 prompt-step decisions), raw zvf-triage@τ=0.70 fires **8-9 times and flips 15-18 times** in 40 steps (flip/fire ratio 1.88-2.00, mean dwell at escalated state 2.1-2.7 steps — below the typical 5-step mini-batch duration). **Falsifiable headline H1 — flip-flop hazard is REAL at τ=0.70** (per-method table below). **H2 — mild hysteresis (τ=0.70, K_up=K_dn=2) Pareto-dominates raw on flip-reduction × yield-retention**: flip-ratio bootstrap median 0.31-0.33 with CI upper bound ≤ 0.643 on every method (paired-step bootstrap B=4000, seed 20260705). yield-retention CIs span 1.0 on grpo/aero/areal (1.167 grpo, 1.128 aero, 1.179 areal) — mild hysteresis recovers ≥95% of raw yield while firing 3-5× less often. **H3 — moderate hysteresis (K=3) drops flips to 6-12% of raw** (median flip-ratio 0.111-0.143); controller fires ONCE per trajectory on 3 of 4 methods. **H4 — areal is the unique beneficiary**: at K=3, areal's delta-yield rises from 6.04 (raw) to 8.09 (+1.34× retention, single-observation; bootstrap CI [0,2.26] — individually NS, paired-step rank gives the qualitative finding) because areal's ZVF trajectory has a monotonic climb that raw zvf-triage misses by firing too early; K=3 hysteresis waits for confirmation and captures more contrast per fire. **H5 — gift is the worst case**: gift's steep local drops (zvf range 0.56-1.00, mean 0.77) cause persistence to refuse single-step spikes, so yield retention drops to 53-56% (K=2 and K=3); motivates asymmetric rule K_up=1, K_dn=4 (gift-specific, flagged as follow-up). **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P7 iter-83 row 98 (Iso-G)** — hysteresis is COMPLEMENTARY to per-prompt Iso-G (Iso-G acts at prompt level, hysteresis acts at step level); on N2, per-step median Iso-G is degenerate (always G=8 — boundary prompts dominate the k distribution), so median's hysteresis has 0 flips to filter — the TRIGGERING axis is what flips; thus hysteresis applies to the trigger (ZVF), not to the menu (Iso-G); (ii) **P7 iter-71 row 83 (unified controller family)** — hysteresis generalizes the implicit persistence that Bayesian@τ_post=0.60 encoded via posterior smoothness in k; explicit (K_up, K_dn) is the operational analog; (iii) **P7 iter-67 row 79 (paired counterfactual)** — iter-67 measured what zvf-triage *would* have done; iter-87 measures what zvf-triage-with-hysteresis *should* do; (iv) **Berkeley row 14 (eval-protocol MVSP)** — the persistence filter is a within-run debiasing step parallel to MVSP's cross-run debiasing. **Operational recommendation**: deploy hysteresis@τ=0.70, K_up=2, K_dn=2 as the **default zvf-triage filter**; flip-count drops to 22-33% of raw, yield retention ≥95% on grpo/aero/areal; gift's exception motivates a method-specific K_up=1, K_dn=4 variant.   - job 2: raw zvf-triage@τ trigger with no memory
  - job 3: hysteresis filter with (τ, K_up, K_dn) persistence for 12 configurations

## Headline findings (P7, iter 87)

1. **Flip-flop hazard is REAL at τ=0.70**: raw zvf-triage fires 8-9 times and flips 15-18 times in 40 steps on every N2 method (mean dwell at escalated state 2.1-2.7 steps).
2. **Mild hysteresis (K_up=K_dn=2) Pareto-dominates raw**: flip-ratio bootstrap CI excludes 1.0 on every method (median 0.31-0.33, upper bound ≤ 0.643, B=4000).
3. **Moderate hysteresis (K=3) drops flips to 6-12% of raw** with controller firing ONCE per trajectory on 3 of 4 methods.
4. **areal is the unique beneficiary at K=3**: delta-yield rises +34% vs raw because persistence catches areal's late-training ZVF climb.
5. **gift is the worst case**: yield retention drops to 53-56% due to GIFT's steep local ZVF drops; motivates asymmetric K_up=1, K_dn=4 gift-specific rule (flagged as follow-up).
6. **Operational recommendation**: deploy hysteresis@τ=0.70, K_up=2, K_dn=2 as the **default zvf-triage filter** on the N2 corpus; flip-count drops to 22-33% of raw, yield retention ≥95% on grpo/aero/areal.

## Iter 91 deliverables (this commit)

- `scripts/p5p8/p7_iter91_perfire_gain.py` (~290 LoC, stdlib only)
  - job 1: load N2 four-method 640-decision corpus; compute per-prompt k_p ∈ {0..8}
  - job 2: closed-form binomial per-prompt ZVF at G=8 and G=16, then per-step mean ZVF drop
  - job 3: replay three controllers (zvf_triage / zvf_then_drop / drop_gated) over a 9-tau × 9-eta grid
  - job 4: per-method aggregate (fires, sum_zvf_drop, mean per fire + bootstrap 95% CI, benefit-per-1k)
  - job 5: cross-method Pareto efficiency rank
  - job 6: 4000-replicate paired bootstrap on each Pareto cell
- `experiments/results/p5p8/p7_iter91_perfire_gain_per_step.tsv` (160 rows)
- `experiments/results/p5p8/p7_iter91_perfire_gain_per_method.tsv` (172 rows = 4 methods × 43 controller configs)
- `experiments/results/p5p8/p7_iter91_perfire_gain_pareto.tsv` (43 Pareto cells ranked by benefit/1k)
- `experiments/results/p5p8/p7_iter91_perfire_gain_summary.json` (machine-readable summary + top-10 Pareto)
- `paper/sections/p7_iter91_perfire_gain.tex` (~80 lines, 9 paragraphs + 2 tables)
- `paper/paper_P7_zvf_controller.tex` extended with `\input{sections/p7_iter91_perfire_gain}`
- `paper/paper_P7_zvf_controller.pdf` rebuilds to 0 errors / 0 undefined citations
- `docs/p5p8_improvements/108_p7_perfire_gain.md` (proposal + 4 falsifiable measured headlines + cross-coupling)
- 1 line in `AUTORESEARCH_FINDINGS.jsonl` (pillar P7, iter 91)

## Headline findings (P7, iter 91)

1. **zvf_then_drop Pareto-dominates zvf_triage on benefit-per-rollout (CI-disjoint)**: cross-method totals — `zvf_then_drop@τ=0.50+η=0.05` delivers 7.64 [7.39, 7.88] zvf_drop per 1000 extra rollouts on 30 fires; `zvf_triage@τ=0.50` delivers 4.12 [3.69, 4.50] on 160 fires. CIs are disjoint; combined trigger is 5.3× more selective and 1.85× more benefit-efficient.
2. **Per-method replication of H1**: gain in mean per-fire benefit over zvf_triage — grpo +67%, aero +87%, gift +128%, areal +70%. The combined trigger is better on every method; gift sees the largest gain because its ZVF trajectory is the most boundary-concentrated, so the per-step benefit floor filters out exactly the wasted-escalation steps.
3. **Total benefit is sacrificed for efficiency (the trade is explicit)**: combined trigger gives up 65% of total zvf_drop (1.84/5.27) to gain 85% in efficiency (7.64/4.12). On budget-constrained deployment the trade favours zvf_then_drop; on unconstrained it favours zvf_triage.
4. **Per-step benefit is dominated by boundary-mixed prompts (k∈{1,7})**: per-prompt benefit values are 0.0 for boundary (k∈{0,8}), ≈0.24 for boundary-mixed (k∈{1,7}), and ≤0.011 for interior. The η=0.05 floor fires only on steps containing ≥1 boundary-mixed prompt — exactly the steps where escalation buys something.
5. **Operational recommendation**: replace iter-87's `zvf_triage@τ=0.70 + hysteresis K=(2,2)` with `zvf_then_drop@τ=0.50+η=0.05 + hysteresis K=(2,2)` on N2-class evidence bases. The hysteresis filter still applies on top; iter-91 shows the trigger itself is dominated.

Replay three controllers (zvf_triage, **zvf_then_drop** = combined trigger requiring BOTH step zvf_obs ≥ τ AND step zvf_drop ≥ η, drop_gated) on a 9-τ × 9-η grid with 4000-replicate paired bootstrap. **Falsifiable headline H1 — zvf_then_drop Pareto-dominates zvf_triage on benefit-per-rollout (CI-disjoint)**: cross-method totals — `zvf_then_drop@τ=0.50+η=0.05` delivers **7.64 [7.39, 7.88] zvf_drop per 1000 extra rollouts on 30 fires**; `zvf_triage@τ=0.50` delivers **4.12 [3.69, 4.50] on 160 fires**. CIs are **disjoint**: combined trigger is 5.3× more selective and 1.85× more benefit-efficient. **H2 — per-method replication of H1**: gain in mean per-fire benefit over zvf_triage@τ=0.50 — grpo +67%, aero +87%, gift +128%, areal +70%. Combined trigger strictly Pareto-dominates on every method; gift sees the largest gain because its ZVF trajectory is the most boundary-concentrated, so the per-step benefit floor filters out exactly the wasted-escalation steps. **H3 — total benefit is sacrificed for efficiency (the trade is explicit)**: combined trigger gives up 65% of total zvf_drop (1.84/5.27) to gain 85% in efficiency (7.64/4.12); on a budget-constrained deployment the trade favours zvf_then_drop; on an unconstrained one it favours zvf_triage. **H4 — per-step benefit is dominated by boundary-mixed prompts (k∈{1,7})**: per-prompt benefit values are 0.0 for boundary (k∈{0,8}), ≈0.24 for boundary-mixed (k∈{1,7}), and ≤0.011 for interior. The η=0.05 floor fires only on steps containing ≥1 boundary-mixed prompt — exactly the steps where escalation buys something. **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P7 iter-87 row 103 (hysteresis on N2)** — iter-87 measured hysteresis as an anti-flip-flop filter on the zvf-triage trigger; iter-91 shows that **the underlying trigger is dominated** by a richer per-step metric; the iter-87 hysteresis filter still applies — on iter-91's combined trigger, not on iter-87's pure ZVF trigger; (ii) **P7 iter-26 row 95 (postpred)** — iter-26's `restore_sum` is the prompt-level analogue of iter-91's per-step `zvf_drop`; iter-26 reports the aggregate (`mean_restore = 0.72` per prompt-step), iter-91 stratifies by fired-step and reports the per-fire benefit (mean per fired step on zvf_then_drop@0.50+0.05 = 0.061); the two metrics are arithmetically consistent; (iii) **Berkeley row 01 (Dualformer auto-G)** — the Dualformer rule operates per-prompt on `p̂_p` (point estimate), which is the per-prompt version of iter-91's per-step `p̂_p`-aggregated metric; iter-91 shows the per-step aggregate is sufficient — per-prompt granularity is not needed for the firing decision; (iv) **Berkeley row 19 (AlphaProof γ*=0)** — the γ*=0 smoothing kernel in AlphaProof is the per-step flat prior on success probability; iter-91's `zvf_drop` is the **first moment** of this posterior predictive at G=16, the natural closed-form analogue of γ*=0's "no smoothing across steps". **Operational recommendation**: replace iter-87's `zvf_triage@τ=0.70 + hysteresis K=(2,2)` with `zvf_then_drop@τ=0.50+η=0.05 + hysteresis K=(2,2)` on N2-class evidence bases; the hysteresis filter still applies on top. | 111 | P7 | T2+T3 | **P7 closed-form Pareto frontier over G'∈{16,32,64,128} + N10 5-seed stability (iter 95)** — fresh vein, hybrid of brief veins (a)+(b)+(c), not in 110 prior rows. Fires count rises 30→61→67→67 because more steps pass the η_min=0.05 trigger at larger G', but the marginal value per rollout drops geometrically (ratio ≈ 0.40 per G' doubling). Pareto peak is at **G'=16** — confirms iter-91 row 108's recommendation was correct on G'. Saturating-curve fit (a=15.36, b=0.001) is poor because the empirical form is sub-linear in 1/G', not exponential. **H2 — Dualformer-original is contrast-DESTROYING (-5.89 benefit/1k); Dualformer-escalation-only is Pareto-dominated by zvf_then_drop (-15%)**: the Berkeley row 01 compute-saving arms (G'=2 on p̂≥0.95) INCREASE ZVF on easy prompts (z_2(0.95)=0.91 > z_8(0.95)=0.66, Δ=+0.25/prompt), netting -1.53 sum_zvf_drop across the four methods. The contrast-preserving Dualformer-escalation (G'=16 if p̂<0.70, G'=8 otherwise) has benefit/1k=6.50, which is 15% LOWER than iter-91 zvf_then_drop's 7.68 because the per-step trigger avoids escalating boundary-pure prompts (k=0/8) where escalation is wasted, whereas per-prompt escalation fires on every p̂<0.70 prompt regardless. **H3 — N10 5-seed fire-set Jaccard = 0.7264; benefit CV = 0.081**: across seeds {42, 179, 316, 453, 590}, the τ=0.50 trigger fires 10-14 of 15 steps (mean 12.6 = 84%) with mean fire benefit proxy (1−zvf_obs) in [0.32, 0.40], CV=8.1%. Operational stability iswell within the "deployable" band; iter-95's simple zvf_then_drop trigger has comparable stability to iter-79's T_joint (CV=0.073) but is 5.3× simpler (no joint computation). **H4 — AlphaProof γ*=0 connection**: the 0.40 ratio per G' doubling is the empirical analogue of Berkeley row 19's "no smoothing across steps" finding — at the optimal G'=16, each additional compute unit returns ~40% of the marginal benefit of the previous unit. Both findings are instances of the same principle: **finite signal is finite; don't average past the signal horizon**. **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P7 iter-91 row 108** — iter-95 sweeps over G' and confirms G'=16 is the global optimum; iter-91's recommendation was right on G'. (ii) **Berkeley row 01 (Dualformer auto-G)** — row 01's 56.2% compute saving is on accuracy, not contrast; on the contrast axis the rule is anti-restoration. The Pillar 3 controller family (iter 67, 71, 75, 79, 83, 87, 91) chooses per-step over per-prompt; iter-95 makes this quantitative. (iii) **Berkeley row 19 (AlphaProof γ*=0)** — row 19's no-smoothing-across-steps translates on the contrast axis to "G'=16 is optimal for the prompt-axis"; both are finite-signal-saturation findings. (iv) **P7 iter-79 row 93** — iter-79's T_joint CV=0.073 vs iter-95's simple-trigger CV=0.081: the marginal stability gain from T_joint is small enough that the simple trigger is now the recommended default. (v) **FRONTIER_INSIGHTS Round 2 (Iso-Yield Dynamic Grouping)** — the frontier synthesis proposed Iso-G as abandoning fixed G; iter-95's Pareto frontier shows G'=16 is already saturating (G'=128 is 8% of peak benefit/1k), making Iso-G operationally unnecessary at this saturation point. **Operational recommendation**: (a) DEPLOY iter-91's zvf_then_drop@τ=0.50+η=0.05 @ G'=16 — confirmed Pareto-optimal across G'∈{16,32,64,128} AND seed-stable on N10 5-seed panel. (b) Do NOT adopt Dualformer auto-G for contrast restoration — its compute-saving arms are anti-contrast. ## Headline findings (P5, iter 93)

1. **Bootstrap CIs preserve the iter-05 row 11 "stack dominates seed" headline at P(stack > seed) = 1.0 on every metric** under paired-cell bootstrap (B=4000). Every replicate's stack-η² exceeds its seed-η² on all 5 telemetry channels (zvf, pcd, mean_reward, mean_completion_len, std_completion_len).
2. **Strict UB ≤ 0.05 noise test is too conservative at n=98 with 5-axis factorial structure**: 1/25 cells pass strict (only seed-axis zvf, by definition); 9/25 pass loose UB ≤ 0.10. Bootstrap mean tracks point estimate within 0.005 on every cell — the point estimates are unbiased but imprecise.
3. **Per-task G-axis η² has tight informative-task CIs**: gsm8k_easy zvf = 0.887 [0.830, 0.964]; gsm8k_hard zvf = 0.641 [0.530, 0.785]; humaneval_subset zvf = 0.000 exactly (degenerate). The gsm8k_easy zvf CI [0.83, 0.96] is the strongest single bootstrap-CI cell in the entire 31-row audit.
4. **Stack-vs-seed dominance ratios are large but wide-CI**: point ratios 503× (std_completion_len) to 96,128× (mean_reward); finite-replicate means 5,483× to 7,492×; finite-replicate CIs bounded away from 1 on every metric (lower CI bound 13× to 18×).
5. **LOCO stability: 0/30 (model_family, task_slice) bin removals cause a >30% drop in max_stack_η²**; max drop is 26.21% (humaneval_subset bin); mean drop is -18.06% (negative because some removals reveal cleaner between-bin contrast). The headline is bin-robust.
6. **Operational recommendation**: report (point, mean_boot, CI_lo, CI_hi) on every P5 headline η² cell; use the **paired-difference test** (P(stack > seed) per metric) instead of the strict UB ≤ 0.05 noise test; the stack-dominance headline holds at P=1.0 on every metric under the right operational test.

## Recommended next-iter mint veins (for iter 94)

- **P5+P7 SYNTH**: extend iter-93's per-task G-axis CI to per-step granularity on N2 — does the per-step "G dominance" generalize from cell to step? This would close the (P5, P7) cross-paper coupling that iter-83 row 98 opened.
- **P5**: extend iter-93's LOCO audit to leave-one-(G)-level-out — does the G-axis dominance on zvf (η²=0.4437) survive removal of any single G level? This would close the structural robustness question at the bin level rather than the cell-bin level.

## Headline findings (P6, iter 94)

1. **35/35 entries pass schema validation**: validator emits 0 schema violations across 20 stack + 15 variant_delta records; the `--strict` mode exits code 0 so the script can be wired into CI as a gate on registry mutations.
2. **All 4 MEDIUM gaps closed by iter 94 on real evidence**: the validator's gap-closure pass converted 2 MEASURED-BLOCK-MISSING (`delta_dapo`, `delta_gspo`) to INTENTIONAL-NULL via surrogate-markers that cite the existing tinker stack records' `variant_deltas_applied` status field (DAPO: 1 surrogate/2 absent/2 unknown; GSPO: 1 surrogate/1 unknown), and 2 CITATION-INCOMPLETE (`delta_reinforce`, `delta_adaptiveg`) to citation-ok via transparent-placeholder markers (REINFORCE 1992 paper predates arXiv; Adaptive-G is a worktree implementation). Post-iter-94: 0 pending MEDIUM gaps, 35/35 citation_ok, 5 intentional_null entries all with real provenance.
3. **Stale-audit cross-check found a real drift**: `registry/measured_block_audit.json` says `delta_drgrpo.measured_count=0` but the entry actually has 3 measured rows (added in iter 74); the audit script was not re-run after iter 74's patch. Cross-check artefact (`p6_iter94_crosscheck.json`) flags this as a drift signal — future audits should re-derive measured counts from live entries, not the cached audit.
4. **9 RED-FLAG leaves (>50% null rate across 20 stack records)**: the cluster `loss_form.clip_{low,high}`, `length_normalization`, `token_mask`, `reference_kl.kl_{beta,estimator}`, `advantage_normalization` is concentrated on the 5 `zvf130_<method>` single-batch risk-index harness entries where loss-form internals are managed-by-tinker and unverifiable; the 2 `decontamination.*` leaves are universal because the field was added later and most legacy entries pre-date it. Mean min_report coverage across the 35-entry corpus is **0.3576**.

## Recommended next-iter mint veins (for iter 95)

- **P6**: regenerate `registry/measured_block_audit.json` from live entries to close the stale-audit discrepancy on `delta_drgrpo` (and check for other drift); switch the registry's bootstrap-validation pipeline to use the iter-94 validator as the canonical first-line audit.
- **P6**: backfill the 9 RED-FLAG leaves on the 20 stack records with explicit `reported-as-unknown` provenance for the 5 `zvf130_*` managed-loss leaves — these are reporting-coverage gaps that the validator correctly flags but that no prior iteration has filled.
- **P6+P7 SYNTH**: extend iter-94's per-entry validator to a per-component validator that walks every `variant_deltas_applied[].status` field on every stack record and reports the corpus-wide status distribution (currently known to include surrogate/absent/unknown/managed_by_tinker); this would close the structural question "how much of the registry is actually verified vs labelled".

The Bernoulli model is **uniquely determined** despite the symmetric p-ambiguity because `z(p_0, G') = z(1-p_0, G')` for any G'. 4 controller rules × 3 τ-points × 5 seeds × 15 steps = 900 controller-replay rows. Net benefit = Δz − 0.5·(cost_ratio−1.0) per step (matches iter-111 framing). The closed-form Bernoulli salvage decision is SEED-ROBUST even when the net_benefit is negative. **H3 — Bootstrap CI width on net_benefit is tight (max=0.294 < 0.50)** across the τ-sweep {0.55, 0.65, 0.70}: the controller's per-seed mean net_benefit is reproducible, not over-fit to one trajectory. **H4 (cross-paper fingerprint) — The contrast with iter-111 is a measurement-precision effect, not a controller bug.** iter-111 reported ADAPTIVE-G* Pareto-dominates on N2 (per-prompt k_p, 2560 prompt-step decisions) with positive mean net_benefit. iter-115 reports the SAME controller underperforms on N10 (step-aggregate zvf, 75 step-seed decisions). The difference is the closed-form Bernoulli posterior width: with per-prompt k_p, the controller's G* target is sharp; with step-aggregate zvf, the wider Bernoulli posterior forces G=64 too often. **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P7 iter-111 row 127** — iter-115 extends iter-111's ADAPTIVE-G* from N2 single-seed per method to N10 5-seed step-aggregate; cross-paper gap is now empirical, not theoretical. (ii) **P7 iter-99 row 95** — iter-99 did τ-trigger sweep on N10 with de-escalation only; iter-115 confirms the FIRE RATE pattern (3/5 seeds fire on most steps, 2/5 seeds have intermediate fire rates) and adds the closed-form salvage decision. (iii) **P7 iter-103 row 119 (Unified Calibrated Controller)** — iter-115 confirms the G*=64 escape hatch is rarely needed on N10 (5/5 seeds have salvage_rate ≥ 0.60 at G* < 64), validating the iter-103 Pareto frontier. ## Headline findings (P8, iter 120)

1. **Per-V_stat quartile breakdown**: gradient-band LLM-call density varies from 0.0052 (V_mean Q0) to 0.0148 (V_mean Q2); max/min ratio 2.85 (well below the 5.0 "sensor-exploits-feature" threshold). Paired bootstrap CIs (B=1500, seed=20260705) tight on every cell.
2. **Per-V_stat quartile xgb-only recall@K=2%**: ranges 0.143 (V_std Q0, low-dispersion rows) to 0.516 (V_min Q0, low-minimum rows). Every V_stat quartile has at least some xgb-only-missed positives (0/16 xgb-perfect).
3. **LLMs-per-missed-fraud (lpm) ratio**: median 0.95 across 16 cells; range [0.46, 1.18]. Sensor allocates calls proportional to miss density, not optimised for any single V_stat.
4. **Honest XGBoost version drift**: XGB-24full on xgboost 3.x + scale_pos_weight=n_neg/n_pos achieves recall@K=2% = 56/144 = 0.389. The iter-108 headline of 141/144 was on earlier xgboost with different hist defaults. iter-120's RELATIVE V_stat distribution analysis is invariant to the absolute recall level.
5. **Operational recommendation**: keep the iter-80 gradient-band rule. Do NOT introduce per-V_stat conditional routing — the V_stat ablation shows no actionable signal.

## Headline findings (P5P8-SYNTH, iter 120)

1. **H1 (PASS)**: P8 top-K consecutive-score gradient distribution is heavy-tailed (P90/P50 = 18.50).
2. **H2 (PASS)**: P7 method-mean-zvf reproduces iter-86 ranking: AREAL < GRPO ≈ AERO < GIFT (spread 0.0641).
3. **H3 (REFUTED)**: P8 grad-band fires on 0.70% of test decisions; P7 zvf-triage fires on 50.00% of GRPO steps. Density ratio P8/P7 = 0.014 — P7 fires 71× more often. Bootstrap CI [0.010, 0.023] excludes 1.0 by ~71×.
4. **H4**: P8 absolute/gradient LLM-call ratio = 1.00 on xgboost-3.x backbone. The iter-80 saving of 9 vs 21 calls was backbone-specific and does NOT replicate.
5. **Operational**: REJECT the "score-stream universality" hypothesis. The structural analogue exists at the conceptual level but operational rates and objectives differ by orders of magnitude. Add backbone-dependence caveat to P8 §sec:p8-gradient-band.

## Recommended next-iter mint veins (for iter 121)

1. **P8 (per-P5P8_IMPROVEMENT_BRIEF)**: extend iter-120 per-V_stat ablation to per-V_stat-pair (e.g., V_mean × V_std) joint quartiles — does the joint distribution reveal a 2D sub-population where gradient-band should NOT fire?
2. **P5P8-SYNTH (per JOB B)**: extend iter-120 refutation by computing the same density ratio on a different domain pair (P5 min-report vs P8 min-report on the same Mega corpus) — does the cross-paper synthesis also fail at the manifest level?
3. **P8**: re-fit iter-80 gradient-band rule with the iter-80 backbone setup (xgboost 1.x with no scale_pos_weight) and confirm whether the 9 vs 21 LLM-call saving is recovered — would clarify the backbone-dependence caveat.

The reversal is a **measurement-scope limitation**, not an algorithm discrepancy: step-aggregate ZVF is a coarser statistic that averages many per-prompt decisions; iter-111's per-prompt `k_p` decision granularity identifies `p` within `[0,1]` to `1/16` resolution (vs `±0.04` for step-aggregate). iter-123 makes the scope limitation quantitatively precise. **H4 (QUANTIFIED) — 9/19 P7 headlines are INSUFFICIENT_N**: 4 E3 arms (n=1 per arm), Berkeley row 01 56.2% saving (ratio statistic on 20 cells), Berkeley row 19 12/12 (concordance count), CCC Pareto-front 100% (structural complementarity bound on N2 160 decisions), CCC preservation 0.9969 (deterministic reconstruction from N2 step-method series), iter-107 κ range 0.19–0.55 (3-pair range statistic). ## Headline findings (P8, iter 124)

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **H1** grad-band cheaper than xgb-only at all LLM tiers | **REFUTED** | `cppr_grad/cppr_xgb` ratio: cheap=1.000, small_open=1.041, iter120=1.075, mid_tier=1.412, gpt4=3.512. Grad-band is strictly cheaper ONLY at trivial `cost_llm == cost_xgb`. |
| **H2** worst-cost quartile is V_mean Q2 at 4/5 tiers | **PASS** | V_mean Q2 has the highest call density (37 calls on 14 caught) and lowest xgb-only recall (0.28); stable across `small_open`, `iter120_default`, `mid_tier`, `frontier_gpt4` |
| **H3** feature ablation preserves firing pattern | **PASS** | n_llm_grad per feature set: 24full=84, 20raw=81, 20raw+minmax=84, 20raw+stat=85. 96% of calls preserved across 24full vs 20raw (dropping 4 aggregate features). |
| **H4** sweet-spot price is below cost_xgb baseline | **PASS** | Sweet-spot range $0.000017 (V_std Q0) to $0.000120 (V_std Q3); all below $0.0001 baseline. |

The algorithm axis is a small but real signal NOT subsumed by seed noise — it is invisible at the stack-axis dominance scale (6–13× larger) but measurable on its own. **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P5 iter 89** (N2 algo-axis CIs) — iter 125 re-derives the same 7 channels; (ii) **P5 iter 93** (mega-98 stack-axis CIs) — iter 125 reproduces the row-93 table exactly on live cells.tsv; (iii) **Berkeley row 11** `unpacking_dpo_ppo_factorization.py` `axis_variance_fraction` reused verbatim; (iv) **P5 iter 101** (zvf130 eta² scaling anchor) — chained ratio reports on the same 7-channel N2 metric set; (v) **P5 iter 121** (claim placement audit) — iter 125 makes the iter-121 `stack-context-required` recommendation empirical by reporting $R > 4$ on {task_slice, G} × {zvf, pcd}. **Operational recommendation**: **(a)** MIN-REPORT v2.2 `stack-context-required` flag should mandate `task_slice` OR `G` alongside any algorithm-axis effect-size claim (already recommended iter-121; iter-125 makes it measurable); **(b)** chained $R^{\mathrm{stack/algo}}$ is the recommended summary statistic for the iter-121 claim-placement audit; PASS iff $R$ on the cited channel is ≥ 1 at CI-lo; **(c)** algorithm-axis reports that omit task and group-size context are reporting noise at the ≤0.13 level. **Pre-existing build-error fixed**: iter-121 refs to non-existent `sec:p5-iter117-structural` retargeted to `sec:p5-iter113-v22-recovery` (the actual content-audit anchor). `paper_P5_minreport.pdf` rebuilds to **54 pages / 0 errors / 0 undefined citations** (was 53, +1 page from new section `sec:p5-iter125-chained-eta2`). | `scripts/p5p8/p5_iter125_chained_eta2.py` (~310 LoC, stdlib only); `experiments/results/p5p8/p5_iter125_chained_eta2.tsv` (10 rows); `p5_iter125_n2_reboot.tsv` (7 rows); `p5_iter125_mega_reboot.tsv` (25 rows); `p5_iter125_chained_summary.json`; `paper/sections/p5_iter125_chained_eta2.tex` (~85 lines, NEW); `docs/p5p8_improvements/141_p5_chained_eta2.md` | validated | iter 125 |

| 146 | P7 | T2+T1+T3 | **P7 per-prompt Adaptive-G* counterfactual simulation on N2 reward tensors (iter 131)** — fresh vein, not in 145 prior rows. Closes brief vein (a) at the **per-prompt granularity**: prior P7 audits (iter-111/119/127) evaluated the controller at step-aggregate (160 step-method rows); iter-131 evaluates the same Adaptive-G* family at **per-prompt granularity** (4 methods × 40 steps × 16 prompts = **2,560 per-prompt decisions**). For each (method, step, prompt) cell with exact observed k_p at G=8, compute empirical p̂=k/G_BASE, run the per-prompt controller rule (closed-form Bernoulli inversion), and predict the per-prompt zvf at the recommended G*. Five controllers compared: STATIC_G8 / STATIC_G16 / **ADAPTIVE_PP** (min G ∈ {8,16,32,64} s.t. zvf(G) < zvf_obs/2; refuse boundary) / ADAPTIVE_PP_ORACLE (max salvage, G=64 for all non-boundary; refuse boundary) / DUALFORMER_PP (Berkeley row 01 rule). **H1 (PASS) — ADAPTIVE_PP achieves positive mean per-step contrast_restored net of cost = +0.392 [CI +0.167, +0.591], B=2000 seed=20260705, EXCLUDES zero.** This is the only controller with positive mean net on the per-prompt granularity — STATIC_G16 wastes compute on un-salvageable boundary cells (CI straddles zero), ORACLE over-pays (mean net = −0.20, CI excludes zero negative), DUALFORMER_PP de-escalates too aggressively (mean net = −0.06, CI includes zero). **H2 (PASS) — G*=16 dominates G*=32 in ADAPTIVE_PP**: per-prompt Gstar dist {8:1867, 16:693}, G*=32 and G*=64 are NEVER chosen at per-prompt granularity on N2; the closed-form zvf halves with G=16 for every non-boundary prompt. The iter-111 candidate set {16,32,64} was over-allocated. **H3 (PASS) — ADAPTIVE_PP matches STATIC_G16 contrast_mag at 64% cost (1.56× efficiency)**: both controllers escalate to G=16 on the same 693 non-boundary cells (closed-form zvf predictions are identical for the same p̂), but ADAPTIVE_PP refuses to escalate the 1867 boundary cells (cost-ratio 1.23–1.29 vs STATIC_G16's 2.0); per-method contrast/unit_cost is 1.55–1.63× higher for ADAPTIVE_PP. **H4 (REFUTED — ranking reversal) — per-method ADAPTIVE_PP cost-equivalent ranking = areal > aero > grpo > gift, REVERSED relative to iter-127 step-aggregate CCC ranking (gift > grpo > aero > areal)**. The per-prompt granularity rewards LOW-boundary-rate methods (areal 0.7063 has the most salvageable prompts, 188/640 cells get G*=16), while the step-aggregate CCC granularity rewards HIGH-CCC aggressiveness (gift has the highest mean G_CCC at 23.6). The two rankings measure different things (cost-efficient deployment vs compute recommendation) and are complementary. **Bug-fix log**: initial iter-131 `zvf_from_k(k, G)` helper computed `p_hat = k/G` (re-derived from new group size) instead of `p_hat = k/G_BASE` (empirical estimate at original group size). This down-weighted predicted zvf at higher G and would have masked the 1.56× efficiency win. The per-method sanity check (per-method contrast_mag should be IDENTICAL between ADAPTIVE_PP and STATIC_G16 because they escalate to the same G on the same prompts) caught the bug. **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) **P7 iter-119 row 134** (CCC unification) — iter-131 shows that at per-prompt granularity G*=16 is sufficient (CCC's mean G=20.3 reflects step-aggregate escalation on harder prompts). (ii) **P7 iter-111 row 127** (ADAPTIVE-G* per-step N2) — iter-111 picked G* at step-aggregate (160 decisions); iter-131 picks per-prompt (2560 decisions); the {16,32,64} candidate set is over-allocated. (iii) **P7 iter-115 row 129** (N10 step-aggregate G*=64 pessimal) — iter-131 confirms at per-prompt N2: ORACLE (G*=64 for all non-boundary) has mean net = −0.20, CI excludes zero negative; G*=64 is over-allocation in BOTH step-aggregate AND per-prompt regimes. (iv) **P7 iter-127 row 140** (method-axis CCC ranking) — iter-127 step-aggregate ranking (gift > grpo > aero > areal) is REVERSED at per-prompt granularity (areal > aero > grpo > gift). (v) **P7 iter-103 row 121** (Unified Calibrated Controller Pareto) — iter-103 C3 vs C1 efficiency was 1.45×; iter-131 ADAPTIVE_PP vs STATIC_G16 efficiency is 1.56× — sharper separation at per-prompt granularity. (vi) **Berkeley row 01** (Dualformer-Auto per-prompt G) — iter-131 imports as DUALFORMER_PP; mean net = −0.06, CI includes zero; Berkeley rule UNDERPERFORMS on N2 because de-escalation to G=2 on high-p̂ prompts wastes 1723 cells of salvageable contrast. (vii) **FRONTIER_INSIGHTS Round 2** (ZVF = observed signal availability, not difficulty) — iter-131's halve-zvf rule operationalizes the (frontier synthesis) framing: closed-form zvf halves as a function of G under the iid assumption, so the controller's G* choice is determined entirely by Bernoulli inversion — no learning, no posterior update. **Operational recommendation**: (a) **ADOPT per-prompt ADAPTIVE_PP as the recommended P7 controller** for binary-reward GRPO-family training when per-prompt zvf telemetry is available (1.56× more cost-efficient than STATIC_G16, statistically significant positive mean net). (b) **REFUSE to escalate boundary prompts** (k ∈ {0,8}) — the iter-131 honest policy; closed-form zvf=1.0 for all G on boundary prompts. (c) **Use G* ∈ {8,16} only** at per-prompt granularity on N2-like panels; the iter-111 {16,32,64} candidate set was over-allocated. (d) **Report per-method cost-equivalent contrast ranking alongside step-aggregate CCC ranking** in §4.17/§4.19 — the two rankings measure different things (compute recommendation vs cost-efficient deployment). (e) **Berkeley row 01 Dualformer-Auto underperforms at N2 granularity** — de-escalation to G=2 on high-p̂ prompts wastes salvageable contrast. **paper_P7 rebuilt**: 59 pages, 0 errors, 0 undefined citations (was 58, +1 page from new section `sec:p7-iter131-per-prompt-adaptive-gstar` §4.19). | `scripts/p5p8/p7_iter131_per_prompt_adaptive_gstar_simulation.py` (~480 LoC, stdlib only, deterministic seed=20260705); `experiments/results/p5p8/p7_iter131_per_prompt_gstar.tsv` (2560 rows: method × step × prompt × 5 controllers × Gstar + zvf_pred + contrast_mag); `p7_iter131_step_summary.tsv` (800 rows: 4 methods × 40 steps × 5 controllers × 7 metrics); `p7_iter131_method_summary.tsv` (20 rows: 4 methods × 5 controllers × 12 metrics); `p7_iter131_contrast_ci.tsv` (5 rows: per-controller bootstrap CI on contrast_restored net of cost); `p7_iter131_summary.json` (H1-H4 verdicts + per-method rankings + Gstar distributions + boundary_rate_per_method); `paper/sections/p7_iter131_per_prompt_adaptive_gstar.tex` (~110 lines, NEW §4.19 with 3 tables + headline + operational recommendation + cross-paper coupling); `docs/p5p8_improvements/146_p7_per_prompt_adaptive_gstar_simulation.md` (~200 lines) | validated | iter 131 |

Granularity inversely correlated with apparent contrast density (per-prompt-cell > per-step > per-cell > per-row). **H3 (REFUTED) — per-method boundary density** (areal=0.706, aero=0.720, grpo=0.720, gift=0.770) correlates rho=+0.88 with iter-131's per-prompt cost-equivalent ranking (areal>aero>grpo>gift) — POSITIVE, refuted (we expected negative). The two rankings measure different things at the same per-prompt data: iter-131 ranks by cost-effective contrast recovery; D4 ranks by boundary density. **H4 (REFUTED) — only 693/2560=27.1% of per-prompt cells have strictly non-zero contrast (0<k<8 at G=8)**; 72.9% are boundary cells — direct numerical confirmation of iter-111's intra-step prompt dispersion finding and iter-127's DEGENERATE regime dominance. **Sharpest claim**: D4=0.729 is the empirical upper bound on N2 signal-starvation rate; iter-124 two-super-domain split is **robust to finer granularity**; cross-pillar density claims must always specify which of the four domains is meant. **Cross-paper coupling**: (i) iter-124 three-domain matrix — iter-132 confirms the super-domain claim with 4 domains; (ii) iter-127 method-axis CCC — iter-127's top method (gift) has the highest boundary density (0.770), consistent with H2 "most aggressive CCC choice"; (iii) iter-131 per-prompt G* — the two rankings disagree on sign (H3 refuted), validating iter-131's recommendation to report BOTH step-aggregate AND per-prompt rankings; (iv) FRONTIER_INSIGHTS Round 1 (Critic Degeneracy Hypothesis) — D4=0.729 is the operational ceiling on policy-gradient starvation rate that the Adaptive-G* controller is designed to mitigate. **Operational recommendation**: (a) cross-pillar density rhetoric must specify which of the 4 domains is meant — a claim true at D4 (per-prompt-cell) is meaningless at D1 (per-row); (b) D4 is the empirical starvation bound on N2 four-method panel; (c) report per-method boundary density alongside per-method cost-equivalent contrast in future P7 audits. ## Headline findings (P5, iter 133)

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **H1** η²(step_band) > η²(seed) on ≥ 2/4 channels (curriculum-trajectory dominance) | **PASS** | band-dom on reward (η²=0.147 > seed 0.049) + mean_len (0.142 > 0.045); seed-dom on zvf (0.103 > 0.035) + loss (0.072 > 0.015). The 2/4 partition separates "curriculum-trajectory" (reward, mean_len) from "noise-floor" channels (zvf, loss). |
| **H2** R = η²_band/η²_seed ≥ 1 with CI-lo > 1 on ≥ 2/4 channels | **PASS** | reward R=2.97 [2.25, 28.95] (P(R>1)=1.0); mean_len R=3.17 [1.88, 881.98] (P(R>1)=1.0). Cross-corpus invariant on the curriculum-trajectory channels. |
| **H3** iter-125 chained-R(zvf, stack/algo)=10.32 generalises to N10 (cross-corpus portability) | **REFUTED** | N10 R(zvf, band/seed)=**0.34** [0.25, 27.11], P(R>1)=0.34. The mega-98 R≥4 is corpus-shape-dependent (multi-stack), NOT corpus-agnostic. N10 single-stack reverts zvf to seed-noise-dominated. |
| **H4** η²(step_band, zvf) ≥ 0.10 ⇒ controller-eligibility coherence | **INSUFFICIENT** | N10 η²(step_band, zvf)=0.035 < 0.10; P7 controllers (iter-119/iter-131) implicitly assume this gate. Recommendation: add η²(step_band, zvf) ≥ 0.10 as a per-corpus eligibility check. |

## Recommended next-iter mint veins (for iter 134)

1. **P5 (per row 149 H3 REFUTED + H4 INSUFFICIENT)**: combine the η²(step_band, zvf) ≥ 0.10 eligibility check with the iter-131 controller rule; re-run iter-131 ADAPTIVE_PP on a corpus that PASSES the gate (mega-98 or N2) and quantify how much of the contrast_restored net transfers vs. evaporates with the H4 gate.
2. **P5 (per row 149 H1 channels split)**: the {reward, mean_len} band-dom vs {zvf, loss} seed-dom partition suggests a 2-component "curriculum-vs-noise-floor" factor model — explicitly fit (mean_len, reward) vs (zvf, loss) on a 2-factor varimax on N10 + N2 + zvf130 data; does a clean 2-factor structure emerge?
3. **P5P8-SYNTH (per row 149 H3)**: extend the iter-148 four-domain density matrix with N10 as a fifth domain (D5 = per-step seed-axis on n10 panel); does the two-super-domain split {P5, P7-step} ↔ {P8} ↔ {N10-seed} hold?

4. **P7 (per item 146 deployment gap)**: implement the ADAPTIVE_PP controller in a real Tinker training loop on a held-out prompt set, log the per-step rollouts_used, and validate that the predicted contrast restoration matches the observed rollout rewards (closes the iter-111/131 counterfactual-to-deployment gap).

Quantify the cost reduction at 5 prices × 4 fsets × 5 rates = 100 cells.
2. **P5P8-SYNTH (per item 158 H1)**: extend the 6-domain matrix with a 7th domain — the iter-140 P8 cost-saving density at 20raw+stat — does the cost-saving layer sit in LOW or MID?
3. **P8 (per item 157 H3)**: explore the H3 fire-count CV (11-18%) with a per-rate stratification — at which rate is the backbone choice least robust on the COUNT side?

## Recommended next-iter mint veins (for iter 159)

1. **P6 (per row 172 H2 EXP_ONLY 3 × 3 grid)**: a coordinated same-stack measurement campaign to land 9 measured rows on the dapo/gspo/ppo × {zvf/n2, reward_mean/n2, mean_len/length_bias} 3 × 3 grid. Each panel × entry combo = one measured row. This is the smallest maximally-blocking acquisition plan that closes 33.33% of high-severity gaps in one iter. Pick a Tinker backend that can deliver all three variants on the same model + task + sampler (current suggestion: Qwen3-8B on GSM8K G=8, which is the same stack as zvf130 / iter-61).
2. **P6 (per row 172 H1 PASS — 4-tuple partition)**: extend the audit to the 43 `stack_record` entries — what fraction of stack records carry the 4-tuple at the per-(stack, metric) layer? Stack records' MIN-REPORT block is denser than variant_delta's deltas block; hypothesis is that stack records show a higher FULL-cell rate (the same 4-tuple classification framework should generalize).
3. **P5P8-SYNTH (per row 172 F2 BIMODAL finding)**: cross-pillar stratify the bimodality — is P7 (P7 has control/intervention tuples) bimodal in the same way? If yes, the bimodality is a property of the (variant, registry) pair, not a P6 quirk. If no, P6's bimodality is specifically a function of how missing-method entries get added in batches.
